南亞科(2408) 
買進, TP 710 (潛在報酬 45.49%) 
買進 
Price(10/13):488 
Target Price：710

展望未來，南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存，2027 年上半年
位元出貨量預期將保持平穩，待 4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後，出貨
量將恢復逐步成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開
始進場裝機，第一階段預計 2028 年底前投片量達 3 萬片，全產
能 5 萬片目標於 2030 年底達成，且擴建嚴格以客戶 LTA 的 AI
專案為導向；此外，公司規劃於屏東科學園區建置3D 晶圓封裝與
測試廠，預計 2028 下半年導入設備，並與客戶合作開發客製化  
AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)與 ASIC 專案，已開始產生部分營收貢獻，長
期目標於 2029 至 2030 年將 AI 基礎設施營收占比拉高至 40%
至 50%；技術面上，次世代 1C 製程預計於 2027 年第一季進行
客戶驗證 。 綜合以上 ， 研究部預估南亞科2027 年 EPS 為 130.72
元。考量目前公司記憶體供給吃緊、需求擴大，有長期合約支持未
來成長 ，且公司具備獨特製程升級與客製化產品線成長潛力 ， 研究
部給予 5.5 倍本益比評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

⚫ 公司基本資料 南亞科(2408.TT)公司簡介 
南亞科技(2408.TT)成立於 1995 年，隸屬於台塑集團，為全球領先的動
態隨機存取記憶體(DRAM)製造大廠 。 公司生產基地主要位於台灣 ， 擁有
多樣化的產品組合，涵蓋 DDR5、LPDDR5/5X、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X、
DDR3 及 LPDDR3 等產品線 ， 產品種類達數十顆 ， 服務全球數百家客戶 。
面對傳統記憶體產業之劇烈循環波動，公司近年積極推動技術升級與結
構性轉型，除中長期積極投入 1B(16 奈米)與 1C(12 奈米)先進製程轉換
及新五廠建置，並深入布局企業級 SSD(eSSD)、SmartNIC、BMC 等
AI 伺服器基礎設施，同時與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)利
基產品，正逐步由傳統大宗商品化DRAM 廠，轉型為具備高附加價值與
高敏捷度的全方位利基型記憶體解決方案供應商 。

2Q26 營運表現全面成長，售價調漲驅動獲利大幅提升 
南 亞 科 (2408.TT) 2Q26 營 業 收 入 為 825.49 億元 (Q+68.17% / 
Y+684.23%) ， 營收展現強勁成長 ， 受惠於平均銷售單價(ASP)季增超過
60%的漲勢，有效抵消了位元出貨量與上季持平的量能限制；而在 ASP 
大幅上漲且成本結構維持穩定的推升下，單季毛利率提升至
79.49%(Q+11.60ppt / Y+100.05ppt) 。雖然員工分紅與銷售研發使營業

3Q26 獲利大幅跳升，低價長約重簽驅動 ASP 季增逾六成 
南亞科(2408.TT) 3Q26 營收 1,336.49 億元(Q+61.9% / Y+611.7%)，雖
然出貨量因公司整體庫存已降至極低水位，呈中個位數季減，但整體營
收受惠於平均銷售單價(ASP)季增超過 60%(年增超過 500%)的強勁漲
勢；ASP 強勢上揚的核心主因，在於早期簽訂的低價長期合約 (LTA)於
第三季集中到期並重新議價(對 ASP 季增貢獻度超過半數)。在 ASP 大
幅 上 漲 且 生 產 成 本 結 構 穩 定 的 推 升 下 ， 單 季 毛 利 率 跳 升 至  
85.7%(Q+6.2ppt / Y+67.2ppt) 。費用方面，因增發員工薪酬獎勵，研發
費用增加至 51.45 億元、銷管費用增加至 24.43 億元，合計營業費用
為 75.88 億元 。 綜合以上 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 3Q26 營業收入為 1,336.49
億元， Q+61.90%/Y+611.70% 、 毛 利 率  85.70% ，
Q+6.21ppt/Y+67.25ppt、 營業利益率 79.98%，Q+6.30ppt/Y+74.02ppt、
稅後純益 876.27 億元，Q+74.58%/Y+虧轉盈、EPS 為 25.40 元。

2026 年受惠 DRAM 報價持續上漲與 AI 伺服器需求，獲利創歷史新高 
展望 2026 年全年，南亞科(2408.TT) 前三季累計淨利已高達 1,639 億
元，累計EPS 達 49.28 元，公司維持 2026 全年位元銷售量成長高十
位數百分比的目標；合約與定價機制上，公司目前長約(LTA)覆蓋率落在 
50% 至 70% 之間，其中 3 年期以上長約占總出貨量 20% 以上，履
約率高達 100%，預期第四季 DRAM 供需緊缺狀態延續，合約平均售
價保持溫和成長；需求端方面，GPU、TPU、CPU 及 ASIC 等 AI 基
礎建設建置強勁，推升 AI 伺服器與基礎設施相關產品之營收占比突破 
20%； 產品組合中 DDR4 與 LPDDR4 合計營收占比超過 60%，DDR5 
占比約 10%。管理層指引目前產業短期內難以緩解供給壓力，隨著第四
季 DRAM ASP 持續看漲 ， 公司毛利率表現有機會再進一步拉升 。 綜合以
上，研究部預估南亞科(2408.TT) 2026 年營業收入為 4,190.95 億元，
Y+529.40%、毛利率  82.61%，Y+60.10ppt、營業利益率  76.83%，
Y+68.96ppt、稅後純益 2,642.16 億元，Y+3,894.93%、EPS 為 76.58
元。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)技術進展與產業影響 
長鑫存儲(CXMT)於 2026 年 9 月宣佈第五代 DRAM 制程(G5)進入量產階
段。儘管目前 G5 產品仍處於客戶驗證與良率提升階段，距離大規模商
業化出貨尚需時間 ， 但已顯示中國DRAM 技術發展速度明顯超出市場原
先預期。 長鑫 HBM3E 良率已達 40%左右。市場普遍認為，長鑫在無法
取得 EUV 設備的情況下，主要透過 ArF DUV 浸潤式曝光搭配多重曝光
(Multiple Patterning) 技術推進制程微縮，因此雖然能夠達到接近國際大
廠先進 DRAM 節點的設計能力 ， 但製造成本 、 良率與生產效率仍與三星 、
SK 海力士及美光存在差距。未來能否持續提升至國際大廠 70%以上的
量產水準，將是觀察長鑫HBM 競爭力的重要指標。短期而言，長鑫技術
進步對台廠商影響有限，長鑫目前仍面臨良率提升與客戶驗證所需時
間，短期內難以快速增加有效供給，因此全球 DRAM 供應緊張的局面仍
將延續至 2027 至 2028 年。相較之下，三星、SK 海力士與美光持續將
產能轉向 HBM 產品，反而造成 DDR4 供給逐漸退出市場，進一步支撐
DDR4 價格維持強勢，有利於以 DDR4 產品為主的南亞科營運表現；然
而，中長期則需注意，長鑫 DRAM 投片量將於 2027 年底達到約 30K 
WPM，並持續擴產，若未來 G5 良率持續改善、HBM 技術逐步成熟，
加上中國市場本土化政策支持，長鑫未來在中低階 DDR5、PC DRAM
及中國本土市場的競爭力可能快速提升 ， 進而對全球DRAM 供需格局產
生實質影響。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)4F²技術 
長鑫存儲在 10 月 12 日有表示年底推出 4F²架構 DDR5 伺服器記憶體，
4F²是將每個記憶體儲存單元配置在面積相當於 4 倍 F 平方的區域內 ， 其
中 F 代表半導體制程的最小線寬，這種設計能讓同一顆晶片容納更多儲
存單元，進而提高記憶體容量。目前，主流商用 DRAM 仍採用 6F 平方
儲存單元結構。長鑫存儲表示，其4F 平方設計的核心，在於自主研發的
垂直環繞閘極(Vertical Surrounding Gate，VSG)電晶體，這項技術透過
垂直閘極架構改善漏電問題，同時大幅縮小單元面積，提高儲存密度。
SK 海力士(SK Hynix) 已於 2025 年公佈 4F 平方技術發展藍圖，另外，
根據南韓媒體今年 4 月的報導，三星電子(Samsung Electronics)計畫於
2028 年前，量產採用這項新設計、制程低於 10 奈米的 DRAM。

成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開始進場裝機，第一

### Page 5

Server TAM estimate revisions

AI server racks: We raise our forecast for AI server racks powered by NVIDIA to 61k / 
97k / 149k in 2026 - 28E (vs. 50k / 92k / 148k previously), reflecting the increase in our 
forecasts for brand makers (Dell, SuperMicro, HP), leading to higher US CSP capex by 4% 
/ 17% / 22% in 2026-28E, and NVIDIA chipset shipments (raised by 9% / 15% in 2027 / 
28E), showing the ongoing upcycle of AI infrastructure. Overall, we expect the AI server 
rack global value TAM to grow at 163% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$1tn, or 63% of the 
global server value TAM by 2028E. By vendor, we continue to expect global leading CSPs 
to be the major customers, with leading ODM direct players as the major suppliers. We 
expect Hon Hai / FII to maintain the leading market position among ODMs and with 
continuous specification upgrade driving its global market share expansion.

AI servers: We raise AI server (8-GPU) equivalent shipment by 10% / 29% in 2027-28E 
to 2.7m / 3.3m in 2027-28E, mainly driven by rising ASIC-based AI server demand. The 
custom-design provides ASIC-based AI servers optimized for AI computation with better 
efficiency, such as lower latency, or memory savings via reduced expensive read/write 
cycles. We continue to see new generation ASICs launched by global leading CSPs, 
supporting AI server growth: we expect the AI servers (8-GPU) global value TAM to grow

at 38% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$318bn, or 20% of global server value TAM by 2028E. 
We reduce 2026E shipment by 7% to 1.7m, reflecting a shift toward higher density AI

General servers: We raise general server revenue by 15% / 13% / 11% in 2026-28E 
driven by high memory costs and ongoing specification upgrades driving AI workloads. 
Intel launched its Intel Xeon 6+ processors at Computex 2026 (link) to facilitate Agentic 
AI workloads, positioning: CPU servers as the control plane, driving system-level 
performance and efficiency under rising agentic AI workloads, while supporting data 
movement and sustained inferencing. Intel presented Xeon 7 Diamond Rapids in Aug 
2026 (link), with architectures for Agentic AI and Enterprise scale workloads, 
empowering efficient inference and edge computing. AMD has begun production ramp 
of its 6th Gen AMD EPYC CPUs (Venice) in May (link), which powers agentic AI and 
diverse workloads - running the agents, AI host nodes, and general purposes that turns 
intelligence into action with optimized TCO (total cost of ownership). We expect general 
servers’ global value TAM to grow at 22% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$271bn, or 17% of 
global server value TAM by 2028E.

US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets by 84%/

### Page 7

Chinese cloud platforms to grow capex by 105%/ 21%/ 14% YoY in 2026-28E, with our 
estimates being raised by 14% / 15% / 11% in 2026-28E. The strong increase in CSP 
capex reflects the high memory costs and growing demand on AI workloads. In terms of 
capex structure, we estimate chipsets (GPU, ASIC, CPU) remain the major contributors, 
along with memory (HBM), and followed by networking and other components (e.g. 
cooling, power, chassis, rail kits, etc.). Compared to global CSPs, we estimate China CSP 
spending on chipsets would be more diversified across GPU and ASICs considering 
various local fabless suppliers and customers’ varying demand on AI scenarios / tasks. 
Read more: Microsoft, Amazon, Meta, Alphabet, Oracle, Baba, Tencent, Baidu.  
Key highlights on the recent Maia 300 announcement from MSFT: (1) Microsoft to 
expand its Azure Maia system to multi-gigawatt deployments, making Maia the primary 
inference system for Microsoft products and services, and (2) Microsoft plans to have 
multi-gigawatt Maia deployment beginning in 2027, with reports indicating a potential 
plan to deploy >1M Maia 300 chips in 2027 (Microsoft is covered by Gabriela Borges).

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or

Realtek
Company description
Founded in 1987, Realtek is a leading fabless semiconductor with
headquarters in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It has an extensive global footprint with
operational offices in China, Japan, Korea, Singapore, and the US. Realtek's
product portfolio is well-diversified; generally, it can be categorized into three
business groups: Communications Network (Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, automotive
Ethernet, PC Ethernet, switch, etc.), Computer Peripherals (audio codec,

clock IC, card reader, IP cam SoC, USB  hub controller, Type-C controller, SSD
controller) and Multimedia (TV SoC, LCD controller, STB SoC, etc.).
Investment strategy
We rate the shares Buy. We believe Realtek is well-positioned to ride on
continued specs upgrade demand for WiFi, switch and 10G PON as well as the
strong advancements in the automotive and Io T sectors. In the long term, we
believe Realtek's cost-effective, high-performance, and comprehensive wired

and wireless solutions should become more critical with the growing
connectivity requirement at the edge.
Valuation
Our target price for Realtek is set at NT$900 based on our target PE multiple
of 20x to our 2027 EPS. We believe our PER of 20x, higher than its 3-year
forward PER average, is justified by its solid growth opportunity in automotive
and switch/PON business. We also believe its continued efforts in products
mix optimization should help to support its margins expansion. At our target

price, the shares would trade at a 2026/27E P/B of 8x/6x.
Risks
Key downside risks to our target price include: 1) Slowdown in technology
migration for its networking business; 2) Weaker-than-expected growth
momentum for automotive Ethernet sales; 3) Worse-than-expected PC/NB
and consumer electronic demand; 4) Slower-than-expected GM expansion
due to pricing competition from peers; and 5) Higher-than-expected memory
cost burden to dampen the order outlook from its networking and PC/NB

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26 년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G 는 +11.5%, ASP 는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G 는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP 는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L 는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic 에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면  이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0% 
(2026.09.30 기준)

Document 265: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2 분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2 분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4 분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0% 
 (2026.09.30 기준)

BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE: DOUBLE
-
DIGIT GROWTH IN BUSINESS CREATIONS,
LOWER GROWTH IN BANKRUPTCIES,
AND STABLE NON
-
PERFORMING LOANS
ECO CHARTS | BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IS A NEW PUBLIC
ATION AIMED AT PROVIDING AN OVERVIEW OF THE DYNAMICS
OF BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE, AS WELL AS THE
RISKS ASSOCIATED WITH THE LATTER. WE WILL PUBLISH
REGULAR UPDATES TO THIS PUBLICATION ON A QUARTERLY BASIS.

-
digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non
-
performing loans

ECONOMIC STUDIES
•
Business
creations
rose
by
12
%
y/y
in
the
first
eight
months
of
2026
,
marking
an
acceleration
compared
with
the
growth
seen
in
2025
(+
5
.
3
%
)
.
•
This
increase
was
driven
by
sectors
associated
with
new
information
and
communication
technologies,
where
the
trend
is
gaining
momentum
.
•
Meanwhile,
business
bankruptcies

rose
by
5
.
3
%
y/y
from
January
to
July,
according
to
the
data
from
the
Banque
de
France
(vs
+
3
.
6
%
y/y
in
2025
)
.
After
a
more
pronounced
rise
in
the
first
quarter
(+
8
%
y/y),
this
increase
has
since
slowed
.
This
moderation
could
continue,
as,
according
to
Altares,
payment
delays
have
decreased
to
an
average
of
12
.
3
days
in
the
second
quarter
of
2026
(
14
.
1
days
a
year
earlier),
compared
with
the
European
average
of
13
.
1
days
.
•
The

rise
in
business
bankruptcies
is
most
pronounced
in
the
services
sector,
which
has
simultaneously
seen
an
increase
in
business
creations
.
This
trend
is
clear
in
the
retail
sector,
with
a
13
%
increase
in
business
creations
since
the
beginning
of
2026
,
while
business
bankruptcies
are
up
3
%
.
•
In
construction
and
manufacturing,
business
bankruptcies
have
been
declining
since
the
second
quarter
of
2026
.
•
The
level
of
the
average
ratio
of
non
-

performing
loans
is
moderately
high
and
has
remained
stable
for
the
past
year
(
4
.
0
%
of

loans
and
advances
in
Q
2
2026
)
.
More
cyclical

in

,
the
ratio
of
non
-
performing
loans
in

reached

2019

9
.
5
%
.
•

share

-

(in

,

9

.
4

-

1

)
.

,

)

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK 
MARKETS IN REVIEW 
Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
I
N THIS  ISSUE
Macro Strategy—The  Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran: Bessent’s Three-Front War and
the Stakes for
Investors: Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury secretary, but
these days he looks more like America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches

from T
okyo, where he is trying to engineer a stronger Japan yen, to the U.S. Treasury
market, where he is battling to keep the 10-year yield from breaking  decisively above
5%, and to T
ehran, where he has become the point man in the escalating U.S. campaign
to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Ho
w Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A decisive victory on
all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets is next to impossible,

while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best laid plans. That said, however,
the U.S. Secretary of the Treasury has signaled that he is ready to take a very  proactive,
pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less encouraging news stems
from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the Treasury secretary.
Market View—Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”: Behind
ever
y Artificial Intelligence (AI)-generated response is a vast physical network of data

centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems, semiconductor facilities
and critical-mineral supply chains. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power,
water, advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and
intensify pressure
on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend
beyon
d AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

2 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.

For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

4 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product
1 BloombergNEF , Data Centers on Track to Suck Up a Fifth of US Power Use by 2035, July 21, 2026.
2 BloombergNEF , AI Buildout Drives 76% Power Bill Jump on Largest US Grid, May 14, 2026.
3 Data Center Watch, Q1 2026 Insights Report, May 2026.

4 Pictet Asset Management, “Sizing AI’s Environmental Footprint,” July 2026.

7 U.S. Environmental Protection Agency, WaterSense, May 2026.
8 Pictet Asset Management, “Sizing AI’s Environmental Footprint,” July 2026.
Investment Implications
Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

6 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,

36% 的營業利潤率已經是 2 個季度⾼於公司整體營業利潤率的 32-34% ，
也明顯⾼於彭博社分析師預期的 32.5% 。⾕歌雲的  Rule of 40% （營收
年增率 + 營業利潤率）在 3Q26 持續增加⾄ 139% ，⾼於 2Q26 的
117% ，及去年同期的 57% 。
No more capex raised: 不同於前幾個季度持續上修資本開⽀，⾕歌
維持  2026 全年 1950-2050 億美元資本開⽀，但 3Q26 季度 514 億美元
的資本開⽀實際上是低於市場預期達 8% ，這讓資本開⽀佔⾕歌雲營收⽐

### Page 3

從 2Q26 的 181% ，降低到  3Q26 的 167% ，即使資本開⽀佔整體營收⽐
仍從 2Q26 的 38% 逐季攀升到  40% 。
Capital return on cloud sales and proﬁts improved
consecutively: ⾕歌雲的資本開⽀營收回報率從上季度的 55% ，增加
到這季度 3Q26 的 67% ，整體營收的資本開⽀的營收回報率從上季度的
46% ，暴增到這季度的 111% 。⾕歌雲的資本開⽀獲利回報率從上季度的
27% ，逐步增加到這季度 3Q26 的 29% ，這表⽰公司的資本開⽀能在 4 年
內透過獲利回收。
Expanding TPUs business- two news in one day
⾕歌宣布  3Q26 ⾕歌雲的營收倍增，除了 AI 基礎建設及解決⽅案外，也包括
TPU 的系統銷售。根據  PR Newswire 正式公告、Oracle 官⽅新聞發布 與
Google Cloud 新聞中⼼，甲⾻⽂（ Oracle, NYSE: ORCL ）與⾕歌

（ Google, NASDAQ: GOOGL ）於  2026 年  10 ⽉  27 ⽇在拉斯維加斯舉⾏
的「 Oracle AI World 」⼤會上宣布  Google ⾃研張量處理器（ TPU ）將正式
進駐  Oracle Cloud Infrastructure （ OCI ）。這是  Google 歷史上⾸次將其核
⼼專屬  TPU 算⼒授權並部署於  Google 體系之外的超⼤規模雲端平台。雙⽅
規劃⾃  2027 年起在  OCI 全⾯商轉第⼋代  TPU 叢集，包含針對前沿模型訓練
的  TPU 8t Zebraﬁsh （算⼒密度達前代近  3 倍，由聯發科提供設計統包⼯

代算⼒以擺脫對輝達  GPU 的單⼀重度依賴。 Alphabet 財務⻑  Anat
Ashkenazi 證實， TPU 外部銷售預計⾃  2026 年底開始認列⼩部分營收，並

Document 355: GPT-6 Astra来了，稚晖君为什么说本体越来越重要_

Source PDF: all report pdf/GPT-6 Astra来了，稚晖君为什么说本体越来越重要_.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

通⽤⼤模型开始伸⼿控制机器⼈，彭志辉的判断是可规模化的优质本体会越来越重要。智
元押注全栈，同时把⾏业的评价标准从下线台数推向上岗⼯时。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 29 ⽇，智元在上海世博中⼼办了第四届创新⽇。主论坛和会后群访⾥， GPT-6 Astra 被反复
提起。
这款通⽤⼤模型最近被证明可以直接控制机器⼈完成⼀些操作任务。市场的疑问很直接，如果
基础模型⾜够强，具⾝公司⾃研的⼤脑还有没有价值，本体会不会沦为代⼯硬件。
智元联合创始⼈、总裁兼 CTO 彭志辉（稚晖君）的回答是，恰恰相反。
他的理由是，不管⼤模型最后能不能通吃具⾝，智能都要落到⼀具可靠的⾝体上才能⼲活。能
低成本、⾼⼀致性、⼤规模造出这具⾝体的公司，并不多。
智元是⽬前跑得最快的⼀家。 2025 年智元出货 5000 多台，按 Omdia 数据占全球 40% ； 2026 年上
半年出货 9700 台， Counterpoint 统计份额超过 43% 。 9 ⽉，第 20000 台机器⼈下线，上半年收⼊
接近 20 亿元。
但彭志辉在群访⾥⾃⼰给这组数字降了温，「万台下线只是制造业的胜利，万台能够上岗才是物

理 AI 的胜利。」
01 ⼤模型越强，本体越重要
本体为什么更重要，彭志辉的推演分两种情况。
⼀种是通⽤基模真的覆盖了具⾝能⼒。⼤家拿到同样的智能，⽐拼的就是谁能以更低成本、更
⾼可靠性把它装进真实世界，规模化本体⼚商占优。
另⼀种是基模做不到真正泛化，各个垂域还要训练⾃⼰的模型。训练数据从部署中来，部署靠
本体，本体⼚商依然占优。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

两条路通向同⼀个结论，可规模化的优质本体会越来越重要。
智元合伙⼈姚卯⻘在主论坛引⽤英伟达的报告， Astra 在 120 项仿真任务⾥平均成功率 66.3% ，能
⼒集中在语义理解和动作规划。但它单次推理要 18 秒以上，涉及⼒触反馈的⾼动态精细动作，
智元测下来成功率基本为零。
彭志辉补充了另⼀个信息，近万亿参数的 Astra 跑在云端，完成⼀个简单任务可能要⼗⼏分钟甚
⾄半⼩时，机器⼈的操作却是实时的。
姚卯⻘的判断是，⼤模型会更多承担 System2 的⻓程规划， VLA 训练出的技能是被它调⽤的⼯
具，再往下是 100 到 1000Hz 的实时电机控制，这⼏层都和本体深度耦合。
本体的作⽤还体现在后训练上。智元在实验室⾥把 100 台真机组⽹，和背后的训练集群实时同
步，机器⼈边⼲活边回传数据，模型权重再下发到每⼀台。同样的训练时⻓， 4 台机器⼈和 100
台机器⼈对⽐，任务成功率从 50% 左右提到 98% 。姚卯⻘的说法是，后训练也有 Scaling ，规模
指的就是真机台数。

这条路，纯模型公司很难复制。
智元两个⽅向都在做。 GO-3 Preview 是⽬前⾏业发布的参数量最⼤的具⾝模型，在 RoboDojo 等
三个评测榜单登顶；同时智元也拿 Astra 当底座，做出了运动智能的⾃进化智能体。
在彭志辉看来，模型是必选题。不做模型，最后就会像传统⾃动驾驶⽅案公司⼀样，被⾃研模
型的主机⼚取代。
这套逻辑有⼀个前提。 Astra 的慢，来⾃参数规模和云端推理，彭志辉⾃⼰也列了解法，分层设
计、模型蒸馏、缓存系统。这些问题如果先被⼤模型公司解决，本体⼚商的窗⼝期会⽐预想的
短。
02 从参数表到⼯时表
客⼾的问题已经变了。
过去看机器⼈，看参数，看跑跳打拳的视频。现在客⼾问的是，它能稳定⼲多少活，⼲⼀次要
花多少钱。
智元给的答案是 Worken ， Work 和 Token 的组合，中⽂名就叫智元，度量机器⼈的有效智能⼯
时。计算拆成三个乘数，在岗活跃台数、⾃主⼯作时间、产出能效⽐。⼀台机器⼈每⼯作 10 分
钟就要⼈接管⼀次，⾃主⼯时就很有限。

### Page 3

收费⽅式上，彭志辉没有给单⼀答案。卖本体、卖服务、和客⼾共创运营都可以，看客⼾要的
是设备还是结果。
真正卡规模的是运维。
彭志辉的原话是，「每卖⼀台机器还要给机器⼈配⼀个保姆，这是永远不会到那⼀天的。」客⼾
愿意⼤规模买单，要等他的问题从「能不能造得出来」变成「能不能养得起」。
⻰旗是⽬前最完整的样本。这条 3C 产线 2026 年 3 ⽉规模化投产，已稳定运⾏ 7 个多⽉， 6 天直播
期间作业成功率 99.99% ，平均节拍 22 秒⼀件，和⼈⼯持平。姚卯⻘透露，智元研发团队早已撤
出现场，基础运维移交给⻰旗⾃⼰的团队， 3C 领域另有 3 家以上企业在合作。
服务场景⾛得更快。横琴⻓隆乐园部署了近 500 台机器⼈，⽇均接待游客超过 1 万⼈，开园后每
周⼀个⼩版本、每⽉⼀个⼤版本地迭代。
⼈机⽐是另⼀条线。最早⼀台机器⼈配⼀个⼯程师，后来 3 ⽐ 1 ，现在 10 ⽐ 1 ，⽬标是 20 到 30 ⽐
1 。⼈机⽐降不下来，每个场景都要堆专家，⽣意就只能按项⽬做。
姚卯⻘给了⾏业层⾯的两个刻度。⼀是部署⼀个新场景要投⼊多少⼈天，他拿⾃动驾驶类⽐，

做得好的团队 10 个⼈ 3 个⽉搞定⼀个⻋型，做得差的要 100 个⼈⼲⼀年。⼆是中国⼯⼚⾥什么时
候有 1 万台以上机器⼈⻓期稳定作业。
Worken ⽬前还是智元⾃⼰定义的⼝径，能不能变成⾏业度量衡，取决于客⼾和同⾏是否愿意按
它结算。
03 壁垒在哪⾥
彭志辉对价格战的判断很明确，本体⾃研和垂直整合能⼒不够，最后只能卷价格、卷成本。
智元的硬件路线⾛了三步。新⼀代 AHA3 架构第⼀次把运动、作业、交互三种智能放进⼀颗
SoC ，控制系统体积降 50% ，通信延迟降 90% 。核⼼零部件⾃研率超过 90% ，但最关键的芯⽚
还不是⾃研，下⼀代 AHA4 会提⾼这部分⽐例。
钱和⼈在往智能倾斜。研发团队 30% 做本体， 70% 做智能。今年训练算⼒接近万卡集群，彭志
辉说，这个规模已经在和通⽤⼤模型公司抢算⼒。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

图⽚
部署端的 Token 成本，他并不担⼼。⼯⼚⼲活⼏乎不耗 Token ，⻓隆这类交互场景耗得多，端侧
⼩模型能闭环掉⼤部分对话，⼤模型再靠蒸馏压缩。在他看来， Token 真成了瓶颈反⽽是好事，
说明客⼾愿意⽤。
场景顺序上，他判断家庭是终点。安全、跨任务泛化、⻓程任务和 C 端定价，要求都⾼得多，⼯
业和商业场景先⾛。
按智元提出的物理 AI 五阶段，可⽤的⾝体已经有了，垂域智能正在部署，下⼀步是形成⾜够泛化
的基座模型。彭志辉认为 ChatGPT 时刻就出现在第三阶段，「应该也很快了」。
那⼀刻到来之前，⼤模型公司和本体公司会在同⼀个问题上相遇，谁能让机器⼈真正上岗。

Document 399: Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

13030420     1

Transformation 
Quantum dictionary, part 1: Decoding the fundamentals 
21 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• What is quantum computing? How does it work? What is a qubit? And when will quantum reach its full potential? Quantum 
computing challenges our intuition and our understanding of what computers can do. In this two-part dictionary series, we 
decode key terms behind what could be the next major technological revolution. 
• Qubits are the basic building blocks of quantum computers, harnessing properties such as superposition and entanglement to 
process information. However, qubits are highly sensitive to their surroundings where disturbances can lead to errors in 
quantum computation, making error correction a key challenge for building quantum computers. 
• There is no single approach to building a quantum computer. Researchers and companies are pursuing a range of qubit 
technologies, from neutral atoms and photons to spin, superconducting, topological and trapped-ion qubits, while quantum 
computing itself is likely to increasingly work alongside classical computing and AI.

First, let’s lay the groundwork… 
Quantum computing  
Quantum computing uses qubits and quantum properties such as superposition (defined later on) to perform complex 
calculations and solve certain problems faster than classical computers.1 From a practical perspective, quantum computing could 
be used to tackle problems related to optimization, machine learning, simulation and factorization across a range of sectors, 
including autos and logistics, financial services, energy and climate, health and life sciences and national security. 
Current quantum computers can perform ‘just’ thousands of quantum operations. As the technology advances from thousands to 
millions, billions and eventually trillions of operations, quantum computers could begin to have commercial applications.  
• Millions: Financial services and security could experience some of the first use cases, such as threat detection, fraud 
prevention, derivatives pricing, bond trading and arbitrage. 
• Billions: Quantum could help power AI through advanced data generation capabilities.  
• Trillions: Quantum chemistry applications could emerge, including drug discovery, battery simulation and materials 
discovery. 
These are the publicly known applications, according to Oxford Quantum Circuits (OQC). And as quantum computing capabilities 
improve, some of these use cases could emerge sooner than anticipated.  
Now, we’ll tackle the core concepts… 
Qubits 
Qubits (quantum bits) are a quantum computer’s basic unit of information. They can exhibit quantum properties such as 
superposition and entanglement (which we elaborate on below) — properties fundamental to quantum computing. 
Quantum gates manipulate the state of qubits, much like logic gates manipulate bits in classical computers. Despite the name, a 
quantum gate is not a physical object, but rather an operation applied to a quantum system that changes its state. 
From physical to logical qubits 
Physical qubits are the foundation of a quantum computer’s hardware. However, they are highly susceptible to error. To address 
this, multiple physical qubits can be structured together to encode a logical qubit, allowing errors to be detected and corrected

1 Schneider, J., & Smalley, I. (2025, June 10). What is quantum computing? IBM. 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

and making the stored quantum information more reliable. In recent years, according to BofA Global Research, logical qubits 
have become a greater focus for quantum computing companies. 
Entanglement 
Entanglement is a condition where two or more qubits become linked so that their states are correlated, regardless of distance 
(Exhibit 1). It allows quantum computers to process information in ways that can be difficult or impossible for classical 
computers. 
So, how does it work? Suppose we have two photons that have opposite polarizations (the orientation of their light waves). 
Before measurement, neither has a definite polarization. When one is measured as horizontally polarized, the other will be 
vertically polarized when measured in the same basis, wherever it is. When measured separately, they appear to be random, but 
when compared, they are correlated (Exhibit 2). 
The challenges 
Once created, entanglement must be protected from decoherence (where interactions with the environment destroy quantum 
correlations). Maintaining entanglement over long distances is another challenge. Photons traveling through optical fiber can be 
absorbed or scattered, making long-distance quantum communication difficult. 2 Quantum repeaters aim to overcome this 
challenge by extending entanglement across a series of shorter links. 
Exhibit 1: Entanglement is a state where at least two qubits become 
correlated 
An infographic of entangled qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 2: Measuring one qubit in an entangled pair instantly 
determines the state of the other, no matter the distance 
An example of what entanglement means for qubit measurement

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Superposition 
Unlike a classical bit, which can be one of two states (0 or 1), superposition allows a quantum system to exist in multiple states 
or configurations at the same time (Exhibit 3). For example, a qubit can exist in the 0 state, the 1 state or any state that is a 
combination of 0 and 1.3  
A quantum system can maintain a superposition until it is observed or measured. Once measured, its superposition collapses into 
a single state. Another way of thinking about superposition is to imagine a sphere with the north pole representing zero and the 
south pole representing one. Other points on the surface of the sphere represent different superposition states. This allows us 
to: 1) represent complex forms of information; and 2) simulate atoms in a computer. 
Schrödinger’s cat 
In 1935, physicist Erwin Schrödinger proposed a thought experiment to illustrate the strange implications of quantum 
mechanics. In this scenario, a cat, a radioactive substance that decays very slowly, and a Geiger counter are put together in a 
sealed box. According to quantum mechanics, the system can be described as a superposition in which the cat is both alive and 
dead until the box is opened. Once the box is opened and the system is observed, the superposition collapses into a single 
outcome.

2 Rehman, M. (2026, May 11). What is Quantum Entanglement? Quantum Insider. 
3 Microsoft. (n.d.). Superposition.

### Page 3

21 October 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

Enabling parallel computation versus classical computing 
Superposition is a key attribute of quantum computing that allows for quantum parallelism, or the ability to perform multiple 
computations at the same time. Classical bits can only be in one of two states, 0 or 1. In contrast, a qubit can be in a 
superposition of 0 and 1, allowing a quantum computer to process multiple possible qubit states at once.4  
Exhibit 3: Superposition is the condition where a qubit can exist in a superposition of 0 and 1 
Illustrating the power of superposition

Source: BofA Global Research 
Note: A Bloch sphere is a geometric representation of qubit states. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Error rates and fidelity 
Fidelity is a measure of how closely a quantum state or operation matches what was intended (Exhibit 4). A higher fidelity 
generally means fewer errors. 
Physical qubits are prone to errors. One way to address this is to combine multiple physical qubits to create a more reliable 
logical qubit. Quantum error correction (QEC) allows errors in the physical qubits to be detected and corrected, helping protect 
the information stored in the logical qubit.

4 Ibid.

### Page 4

4 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: Fidelity measures how accurately a qubit retains its intended quantum state during computation 
Infographic showing the implications of high versus low fidelity

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
How does quantum error correction work? 
At its simplest, QEC relies on redundancy, or storing information across multiple qubits so that errors can be detected and 
corrected.  
Consider an example: suppose someone wants to send a message represented by 0. Instead of sending a single 0, the message 
could be repeated three times as 000, known as a codeword. If one bit were accidentally flipped from 0 to 1 during transmission, 
the recipient might receive 010. Because two of the three bits are still 0, the recipient could infer that the intended message 
was 000. 
However, if two bits flipped, producing 110 or 011, the recipient could infer the wrong message. If all three flipped to 111, the 
recipient would have no way to know from the received codeword that errors had occurred.5 This approach is known as a 
repetition code and illustrates how redundancy can help protect information from errors.  
Repetition codes are a simple form of error correction that can address bit-flip errors, where a 0 changes to a 1 or vice versa. 
More sophisticated QEC codes are needed to address other quantum errors, such as phase flips.6 Shor’s code, developed by 
American theoretical computer scientist Peter Shor, uses nine physical qubits to encode one logical qubit and can correct one 
bit-flip error or one phase flip error, but it can’t correct both types of errors at the same time. Meanwhile, Steane’s seven-qubit 
code, developed by Oxford physicist Andrew Steane, can also correct both types of errors. It has the advantage of being fault-
tolerant, meaning the error-correction process itself does not introduce additional errors.7 
At what error level might we start seeing quantum advantage? 
Some classical computing systems today operate with extremely high reliability, often described as having "17 nines" of fidelity 
(99.99999999999999999%). By comparison, quantum computers currently achieve fidelity rates of around 99.9%. Gerald 
Mullally, CEO of OQC, has argued that quantum systems would need to add roughly three more nines of fidelity to achieve 
commercial relevance, or quantum advantage. 
Noise and decoherence 
Noise refers to unwanted disturbances that affect quantum systems and can lead to errors in computations (Exhibit 5). It can 
come from sources such as interactions with the environment, electromagnetic interference and temperature fluctuations. 
Coherence is a quantum system’s ability to maintain its quantum properties, including superposition and entanglement. When 
coherence is lost through interactions with the environment, the process is known as “decoherence,” causing the system to 
behave more classically (Exhibit 6).8

5 Microsoft Ignite. (n.d.) Introduction to quantum error correction. 
6 Ibid. 
7 Ibid. 
8 Quandela. (n.d.) Quantum Decoherence.

### Page 5

21 October 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

To fully control qubits for calculations and information transfer, they need to be carefully protected from their surroundings. 
External factors like noise, energy, changes in Earth’s magnetic field, radiation from Wi-Fi or mobile phones or cosmic rays can 
interfere with the process. Even small disturbances can lead to decoherence, making it difficult to develop large-scale, fault-
tolerant quantum computers.9 
According to QuEra Computing, quantum noise can be mitigated through physical isolation of qubits, the development of more 
precise control techniques and quantum error correction, which helps detect and correct errors in physical qubits.  
Exhibit 5: Noise refers to unwanted disturbances, e.g., interactions 
with the environment, that lead to errors in quantum computation 
An infographic explaining quantum noise

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 6: Decoherence is when qubits lose their key quantum 
properties, superposition and entanglement 
An infographic of decoherence

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Quantum processing unit (QPU) 
Referred to as the brain of the quantum computer, the QPU is the core hardware where qubits reside and where quantum 
computations take place. QPUs rely on quantum behaviors like superposition and entanglement to tackle problems in areas such 
as cryptography, simulation and optimization. Just as a CPU (central processing unit) in classical computing contains multiple 
components, a QPU includes quantum gates (operations that manipulate qubits in a controlled way, similar to logic gates in 
classical computing) and interconnects, which enable communication between qubits and other quantum processors.10 
Next, we’ll look at broader computing architecture… 
Heterogeneous computing  
Heterogeneous computing refers to a future computing landscape likely combining classical and quantum computing, potentially 
unlocking more powerful, faster and cheaper compute and helping solve problems that classical computers cannot. According to 
BofA Global Research, AI and quantum computing could also converge and complement each other, enabling more 
transformative use cases. 
Quantum modality  
A quantum modality refers to the type of physical technology used to create and manipulate qubits. We discuss the modalities in 
greater depth in the following section. 
Lastly, we outline qubit technologies… 
Neutral atom qubit 
A quantum modality that uses individual atoms with no electrical charge as qubits (Exhibit 7). The atoms are held in place using 
focused lasers known as optical tweezers. Neutral atom qubit systems do not require atoms to be ionized, which means fewer 
energy constraints and potentially simpler cooling requirements. Atoms such as rubidium or cesium are typically used. The qubits 
are manipulated by changing the energy levels or positions of the trapped atoms using laser pulses.

9 QuEra. (n.d.) Quantum Noise. 
10 SpinQ. (2025, January 16). What does a Quantum Computer Look Like? Explain Key Parts.

### Page 6

6 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 7: A quantum modality where atoms with a net electrical charge of zero are the foundation for qubits 
Key features of neutral atoms

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Photonic qubit 
A quantum modality that uses particles of light, known as photons, to carry quantum information (Exhibit 8).11 Optical 
components such as beam splitters, which divide light along different paths, and phase shifters, which change the phase of light, 
can be used to perform quantum operations. Photonic quantum computers can use integrated photonic chips to manipulate 
photons and enable quantum logic. 
Exhibit 8: A quantum modality relying on the quantum properties of light 
Key features of photonic qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

11 Moore, J. (2025, March 28). The 6 different types of quantum computing technology. TechTarget.

### Page 7

21 October 2026    7

I NSTI TUTE

Spin qubit 
A quantum modality that uses the intrinsic spin of electrons or nuclei in solid-state materials, such as silicon or diamond. These 
spins can be manipulated using magnetic or electric fields, and their states are read using optical or charge-sensing techniques. 
Spin qubits are particularly appealing because they can leverage existing CMOS (complementary metal-oxide semiconductor; a 
technology to build processors) manufacturing infrastructure, making it easier to integrate them with conventional processors. 
Superconducting qubit 
A quantum modality in which electronic circuits create qubits. They are built using Josephson junctions, where two 
superconductors are separated by a thin insulating layer. Microwave pulses manipulate the qubits’ quantum states to run 
computations.12  
Specifically, a resistance-free current oscillates back and forth around a quantum loop. An injected microwave signal excites the 
current into superposition states.13 To enable this process, superconducting materials are cooled to near absolute zero (around 
10-20 millikelvin) using dilution refrigerators, helping reduce thermal noise and maintain coherence. 
Topological qubit 
A quantum modality that aims to encode quantum information using topological properties rather than relying only on individual 
particle states (Exhibit 9). One way to think about this is through the paths certain quasiparticles take as they move around one 
another, creating “braids” in space-time. Because the information can depend on the overall pattern of these braids rather than 
small details of their paths, the approach is designed to be more resilient to certain disturbances. 
Exhibit 9: Resilient to small perturbations but requires special quasiparticles not yet been conclusively demonstrated in nature 
An infographic of topological qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Trapped-ion qubit  
A quantum modality in which ions (electrically charged atoms) are held in place using electromagnetic fields. These ions act as 
the qubits and are controlled by the system with tightly focused laser beams.14 The qubits are held inside ultra-high vacuum 
(UHV) chambers.

12 Ibid. 
13 Simon Benjamin 
14 Moore, J. (2025, March 28). The 6 different types of quantum computing technology. TechTarget.

Document 429: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.

Document 445: US Economy Continues To Roar, While The Fed Waits For More
